(1) de_bvl2017_pb_cosmetics_general_2000 (2 mg/kg), de_bvl2017_cd_cosmetics_general_100 (0.1), de_bvl2017_tas_cosmetics_general_500 (0.5) and de_bvl2017_thg_cosmetics_general_100 (0.1): Germany BVL 2017 orientation values for technically avoidable trace content, register status 'guidance impurity limit', not statutory maximum levels, and therefore not selectable under Method v2.0. (2) hc_cosmetic_cd_3ppm (3 mg/kg): a Health Canada cosmetic impurity guidance limit, excluded for the same reason, and adopting it for cadmium alone would additionally mix regimes. (3) eu_2018_1513_pb_consumer_textiles_1mgkg, eu_2018_1513_cd_consumer_textiles_1mgkg and eu_2018_1513_tas_consumer_textiles_1mgkg (1 mg/kg each): the EU consumer-textile restriction, which governs the FABRIC and not the product applied to it, on a homogeneous-material-after-extraction basis rather than this row's product-content basis; carried as a downstream constraint and companion limit, and blocked from anchoring by the row's basis_guard. The audit_exclude_scope on this row removes only the oral/mucosal toothpaste tier, tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures, and colour-additive and colourant specifications; against the register as built it suppresses no row that the audit scope would otherwise surface, so nothing was excluded to make this list shorter. (4) Two further stricter rows exist that the compiler's audit safety-net structurally CANNOT surface, because it filters to the credible-regulator set, and they are therefore recorded by hand rather than left invisible. asean2019_th_cd_cosmetic_products_3000 (Thailand, cadmium 3 mg/kg) is a guidance impurity limit and falls to the same reasoning as the Health Canada value. eaeu_trcu009_2011_pb_natural_raw_material_cosmetics_5mgkg (EAEU, lead 5 mg/kg) is the only STATUTORY row anywhere in the consumer-chemical product-content basis family that is stricter than an anchor adopted here, and it is not adopted for three reasons: the EAEU is not in the HMTc credible-regulator set and admitting it is a governance ruling outside this derivation; its scope is formulation-conditioned (perfumery-cosmetic products containing more than 1 per cent natural plant or natural mineral raw material) rather than the general product tier, and that condition would be satisfied by some but not all rows in this category, which would fracture the category on a formulation test no sovereign wrote for laundry products; and the EAEU instrument does not dominate the adopted regime, since it is stricter on lead (5 against 10 mg/kg) but LOOSER on total arsenic (5 against 2 mg/kg), identical on mercury and silent on cadmium, so taking its lead value while keeping the Chinese arsenic value would compose a ceiling no sovereign enforces, which is precisely what the single-coherent-regime discipline exists to prevent. This enumeration is the output of a credible-set-blind sweep of every one of the 80 register rows the basis_guard admits, not of the automated scan alone, and it is complete as of this build. Verification method: total nickel by microwave-assisted closed-vessel acid digestion (HNO3/H2O2, with a hydrofluoric-acid or lithium-metaborate fusion step where an aluminosilicate, silicate or clay mineral phase is present) followed by ICP-MS quantification against matrix-matched calibration standards, with a certified reference material and a reagent blank in every run. The sampling unit is one composite sample per production lot, drawn from the product as placed on the market, with component-level sampling of the bleaching concentrate and, for unit-dose or tablet formats, the film, coating or wrapper, each sampled and tested as a distinct component. A lot at or above the published ceiling fails; a lot inside the ceiling but outside the running per-row control band is escalated to ALARA review with raw-material trace-back.

**Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** No sovereign government sets a lead maximum for a laundry or fabric-care product. Instruments checked: China GB 14930.1-2022 (the only sovereign detergent contaminant standard in the register; scope is Type A detergents used directly for food cleaning and Type B detergents for tableware and food-contact tools, equipment, packaging and containers, and it sets a total-arsenic ceiling only); China NMPA 2015 Safety and Technical Standards for Cosmetics Table 2 (lead, arsenic, mercury, cadmium); Korea MFDS Notice 2025-63 and its predecessor Notice 2024-9 (lead, cadmium, nickel, antimony, arsenic, mercury); Germany BVL 2017 technically-avoidable heavy metals in cosmetics (lead, cadmium, mercury, arsenic, antimony); Health Canada cosmetic heavy-metal impurity limits; the ASEAN Cosmetic Directive Annex (2019) and the Malaysia NPRA, Singapore HSA, Indonesia BPOM, Taiwan MoHW and New Zealand cosmetic group-standard equivalents; EAEU TR CU 009/2011; EU REACH Annex XVII entry 20 (organotin), entry 23 (cadmium in listed polymers and paints), entry 47 as amended by Regulation (EU) 301/2014 (Cr(VI) in skin-contact leather), entry 72 with Appendix 12 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 (lead, cadmium, arsenic and Cr(VI) in consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles) and entry 75 under Regulation (EU) 2020/2081 (tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures); EU Directive 2009/48/EC toy-safety migration limits; EU RoHS 2011/65/EU; and the dietary contaminant regimes (EU 2023/915, EU 2024/1987, Codex CXS 193, US FDA, FSANZ, Health Canada, UK, Hong Kong Cap. 132V), which are inapplicable both on scope and on basis. China GB 14930.1-2022 does not reach this product form and carries no lead row in any event (its grouped heavy-metals line is expressed as combined heavy metals calculated as lead and is not loaded as a lead limit). The sector regimes — Green Seal GS-48 and GS-51 for laundry care products, the EPA Safer Choice programme and its Design for the Environment standard, the EU Ecolabel for detergents and cleaning products (Commission Decisions (EU) 2017/1214 to 2017/1219 and the 2024 user manual, with the JRC 2025 revision in progress), California SB 258 (the Cleaning Product Right to Know Act) and the New York DEC cleansing-product disclosure best-management practice — name lead as a substance of concern for laundry care but set no numeric ceiling, so they establish materiality and supply no number. A single coherent sovereign regime is read across per analyte rather than the lowest number picked across regimes, which would build a ceiling no sovereign enforces. China NMPA 2015 Table 2 is the strictest coherent STATUTORY consumer-chemical regime for lead, cadmium, total arsenic and total mercury (Korea MFDS is looser on lead at 20 and on total arsenic at 10 mg/kg; ASEAN, Health Canada and BVL are guidance), and it is already the read-across anchor of the live Category 12 (Household Cleaning and Dishwashing) standard for the identical logical move. Nickel is not regulated by the Chinese instrument at all, so for nickel the nearest applicable sovereign consumer-chemical value is the Korean statutory limit; that is the only regime in the register that sets one, and no number in this row was chosen by comparing outcomes. The Germany BVL 2017 values that the audit scan surfaces as stricter are orientation values for technically avoidable trace content, carried in the register with status 'guidance impurity limit' rather than as statutory maximum levels; Method v2.0 selects the strictest applicable MAXIMUM LEVEL set by a credible sovereign government, so a guidance orientation value is not selectable as an anchor. The same reasoning excludes the Health Canada cosmetic impurity limits and the ASEAN, Malaysia and Singapore guidance values. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 72 with Appendix 12 restricts lead, cadmium, total arsenic and hexavalent chromium to 1 mg/kg in consumer clothing, related accessories, textiles with skin contact similar to clothing, and footwear. That instrument governs the FABRIC and not the product applied to it, and it is expressed on a homogeneous-material-after-extraction basis rather than on the product-content basis of this category. It is disclosed here as a related but distinct scope and as the downstream constraint a certified product must not cause the treated textile to breach; it is never borrowed as this category's ceiling, and the row's basis_guard makes that borrowing impossible in the compiler. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 75 (Regulation (EU) 2020/2081) sets the strictest sovereign consumer-mixture metal limits in the register (lead 0.7, cadmium 0.5, total arsenic 0.5, mercury 0.5, Cr(VI) 0.5 and nickel 5 mg/kg), but its scope is mixtures for tattooing and permanent make-up, an intradermal-injection route. Reading an injected-mixture ceiling across to a rinse-off or fabric-deposited topical product would conflate exposure routes, and its register basis is a tattoo/permanent-make-up mixture basis that this row's basis_guard excludes. Lead is measured in this product class in the routed corpus, so a not-material determination is unavailable on the facts. Complete disposition of the stricter rows the compiler's audit safety-net surfaces for this row, none of which is adopted. (1) de_bvl2017_pb_cosmetics_general_2000 (2 mg/kg), de_bvl2017_cd_cosmetics_general_100 (0.1), de_bvl2017_tas_cosmetics_general_500 (0.5) and de_bvl2017_thg_cosmetics_general_100 (0.1): Germany BVL 2017 orientation values for technically avoidable trace content, register status 'guidance impurity limit', not statutory maximum levels, and therefore not selectable under Method v2.0. (2) hc_cosmetic_cd_3ppm (3 mg/kg): a Health Canada cosmetic impurity guidance limit, excluded for the same reason, and adopting it for cadmium alone would additionally mix regimes. (3) eu_2018_1513_pb_consumer_textiles_1mgkg, eu_2018_1513_cd_consumer_textiles_1mgkg and eu_2018_1513_tas_consumer_textiles_1mgkg (1 mg/kg each): the EU consumer-textile restriction, which governs the FABRIC and not the product applied to it, on a homogeneous-material-after-extraction basis rather than this row's product-content basis; carried as a downstream constraint and companion limit, and blocked from anchoring by the row's basis_guard. The audit_exclude_scope on this row removes only the oral/mucosal toothpaste tier, tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures, and colour-additive and colourant specifications; against the register as built it suppresses no row that the audit scope would otherwise surface, so nothing was excluded to make this list shorter. (4) Two further stricter rows exist that the compiler's audit safety-net structurally CANNOT surface, because it filters to the credible-regulator set, and they are therefore recorded by hand rather than left invisible. asean2019_th_cd_cosmetic_products_3000 (Thailand, cadmium 3 mg/kg) is a guidance impurity limit and falls to the same reasoning as the Health Canada value. eaeu_trcu009_2011_pb_natural_raw_material_cosmetics_5mgkg (EAEU, lead 5 mg/kg) is the only STATUTORY row anywhere in the consumer-chemical product-content basis family that is stricter than an anchor adopted here, and it is not adopted for three reasons: the EAEU is not in the HMTc credible-regulator set and admitting it is a governance ruling outside this derivation; its scope is formulation-conditioned (perfumery-cosmetic products containing more than 1 per cent natural plant or natural mineral raw material) rather than the general product tier, and that condition would be satisfied by some but not all rows in this category, which would fracture the category on a formulation test no sovereign wrote for laundry products; and the EAEU instrument does not dominate the adopted regime, since it is stricter on lead (5 against 10 mg/kg) but LOOSER on total arsenic (5 against 2 mg/kg), identical on mercury and silent on cadmium, so taking its lead value while keeping the Chinese arsenic value would compose a ceiling no sovereign enforces, which is precisely what the single-coherent-regime discipline exists to prevent. This enumeration is the output of a credible-set-blind sweep of every one of the 80 register rows the basis_guard admits, not of the automated scan alone, and it is complete as of this build. Verification method: total lead by microwave-assisted closed-vessel acid digestion (HNO3/H2O2, with a hydrofluoric-acid or lithium-metaborate fusion step where an aluminosilicate, silicate or clay mineral phase is present) followed by ICP-MS quantification against matrix-matched calibration standards, with a certified reference material and a reagent blank in every run. The sampling unit is one composite sample per production lot, drawn from the product as placed on the market, with component-level sampling of for liquids, the finished emulsion; for dryer sheets, the nonwoven substrate and the applied softener coating, each sampled and tested as a distinct component, because the substrate and the coating have separate supply chains and separate metal profiles. A lot at or above the published ceiling fails; a lot inside the ceiling but outside the running per-row control band is escalated to ALARA review with raw-material trace-back.

The sampling unit is one composite sample per production lot, drawn from the product as placed on the market, with component-level sampling of for liquids, the finished emulsion; for dryer sheets, the nonwoven substrate and the applied softener coating, each sampled and tested as a distinct component, because the substrate and the coating have separate supply chains and separate metal profiles. A lot at or above the published ceiling fails; a lot inside the ceiling but outside the running per-row control band is escalated to ALARA review with raw-material trace-back.

  - **tHg substantiation:** No sovereign government sets a mercury maximum for a laundry or fabric-care product. Instruments checked: China GB 14930.1-2022 (the only sovereign detergent contaminant standard in the register; scope is Type A detergents used directly for food cleaning and Type B detergents for tableware and food-contact tools, equipment, packaging and containers, and it sets a total-arsenic ceiling only); China NMPA 2015 Safety and Technical Standards for Cosmetics Table 2 (lead, arsenic, mercury, cadmium); Korea MFDS Notice 2025-63 and its predecessor Notice 2024-9 (lead, cadmium, nickel, antimony, arsenic, mercury); Germany BVL 2017 technically-avoidable heavy metals in cosmetics (lead, cadmium, mercury, arsenic, antimony); Health Canada cosmetic heavy-metal impurity limits; the ASEAN Cosmetic Directive Annex (2019) and the Malaysia NPRA, Singapore HSA, Indonesia BPOM, Taiwan MoHW and New Zealand cosmetic group-standard equivalents; EAEU TR CU 009/2011; EU REACH Annex XVII entry 20 (organotin), entry 23 (cadmium in listed polymers and paints), entry 47 as amended by Regulation (EU) 301/2014 (Cr(VI) in skin-contact leather), entry 72 with Appendix 12 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 (lead, cadmium, arsenic and Cr(VI) in consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles) and entry 75 under Regulation (EU) 2020/2081 (tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures); EU Directive 2009/48/EC toy-safety migration limits; EU RoHS 2011/65/EU; and the dietary contaminant regimes (EU 2023/915, EU 2024/1987, Codex CXS 193, US FDA, FSANZ, Health Canada, UK, Hong Kong Cap. 132V), which are inapplicable both on scope and on basis. The adopted value sits on an unusually broad sovereign consensus: the Chinese statutory limit, the Korean statutory limit, the Health Canada guidance limit, the ASEAN Annex, the Indian Cosmetics Rules 2020 finished-cosmetic limit and the Hong Kong Mercury Control Ordinance trace-quantity exception all converge on 1 mg/kg for mercury in a finished consumer-chemical product, so the read-across does not depend on the choice of regime. A single coherent sovereign regime is read across per analyte rather than the lowest number picked across regimes, which would build a ceiling no sovereign enforces. China NMPA 2015 Table 2 is the strictest coherent STATUTORY consumer-chemical regime for lead, cadmium, total arsenic and total mercury (Korea MFDS is looser on lead at 20 and on total arsenic at 10 mg/kg; ASEAN, Health Canada and BVL are guidance), and it is already the read-across anchor of the live Category 12 (Household Cleaning and Dishwashing) standard for the identical logical move. Nickel is not regulated by the Chinese instrument at all, so for nickel the nearest applicable sovereign consumer-chemical value is the Korean statutory limit; that is the only regime in the register that sets one, and no number in this row was chosen by comparing outcomes. The Germany BVL 2017 values that the audit scan surfaces as stricter are orientation values for technically avoidable trace content, carried in the register with status 'guidance impurity limit' rather than as statutory maximum levels; Method v2.0 selects the strictest applicable MAXIMUM LEVEL set by a credible sovereign government, so a guidance orientation value is not selectable as an anchor. The same reasoning excludes the Health Canada cosmetic impurity limits and the ASEAN, Malaysia and Singapore guidance values. Deliberate-use mercury allowances for eye-area preservatives (India, New Zealand, South Africa) are not contaminant limits and are not read across. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 75 (Regulation (EU) 2020/2081) sets the strictest sovereign consumer-mixture metal limits in the register (lead 0.7, cadmium 0.5, total arsenic 0.5, mercury 0.5, Cr(VI) 0.5 and nickel 5 mg/kg), but its scope is mixtures for tattooing and permanent make-up, an intradermal-injection route. Reading an injected-mixture ceiling across to a rinse-off or fabric-deposited topical product would conflate exposure routes, and its register basis is a tattoo/permanent-make-up mixture basis that this row's basis_guard excludes. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 72 Appendix 12 sets no mercury row for consumer textiles, so no downstream fabric ceiling is available for this analyte. Mercury is named for this product class by greenseal2020-gs51-laundry-care-products, nydec2020-cleansing-product-disclosure-bmp and epa2024-safer-choice-standard-dfe, so a not-material determination is unavailable on the facts. Complete disposition of the stricter rows the compiler's audit safety-net surfaces for this row, none of which is adopted. (1) de_bvl2017_pb_cosmetics_general_2000 (2 mg/kg), de_bvl2017_cd_cosmetics_general_100 (0.1), de_bvl2017_tas_cosmetics_general_500 (0.5) and de_bvl2017_thg_cosmetics_general_100 (0.1): Germany BVL 2017 orientation values for technically avoidable trace content, register status 'guidance impurity limit', not statutory maximum levels, and therefore not selectable under Method v2.0. (2) hc_cosmetic_cd_3ppm (3 mg/kg): a Health Canada cosmetic impurity guidance limit, excluded for the same reason, and adopting it for cadmium alone would additionally mix regimes. (3) eu_2018_1513_pb_consumer_textiles_1mgkg, eu_2018_1513_cd_consumer_textiles_1mgkg and eu_2018_1513_tas_consumer_textiles_1mgkg (1 mg/kg each): the EU consumer-textile restriction, which governs the FABRIC and not the product applied to it, on a homogeneous-material-after-extraction basis rather than this row's product-content basis; carried as a downstream constraint and companion limit, and blocked from anchoring by the row's basis_guard. The audit_exclude_scope on this row removes only the oral/mucosal toothpaste tier, tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures, and colour-additive and colourant specifications; against the register as built it suppresses no row that the audit scope would otherwise surface, so nothing was excluded to make this list shorter. (4) Two further stricter rows exist that the compiler's audit safety-net structurally CANNOT surface, because it filters to the credible-regulator set, and they are therefore recorded by hand rather than left invisible. asean2019_th_cd_cosmetic_products_3000 (Thailand, cadmium 3 mg/kg) is a guidance impurity limit and falls to the same reasoning as the Health Canada value. eaeu_trcu009_2011_pb_natural_raw_material_cosmetics_5mgkg (EAEU, lead 5 mg/kg) is the only STATUTORY row anywhere in the consumer-chemical product-content basis family that is stricter than an anchor adopted here, and it is not adopted for three reasons: the EAEU is not in the HMTc credible-regulator set and admitting it is a governance ruling outside this derivation; its scope is formulation-conditioned (perfumery-cosmetic products containing more than 1 per cent natural plant or natural mineral raw material) rather than the general product tier, and that condition would be satisfied by some but not all rows in this category, which would fracture the category on a formulation test no sovereign wrote for laundry products; and the EAEU instrument does not dominate the adopted regime, since it is stricter on lead (5 against 10 mg/kg) but LOOSER on total arsenic (5 against 2 mg/kg), identical on mercury and silent on cadmium, so taking its lead value while keeping the Chinese arsenic value would compose a ceiling no sovereign enforces, which is precisely what the single-coherent-regime discipline exists to prevent. This enumeration is the output of a credible-set-blind sweep of every one of the 80 register rows the basis_guard admits, not of the automated scan alone, and it is complete as of this build. The sampling unit is one composite sample per production lot, drawn from the product as placed on the market, with component-level sampling of for liquids, the finished emulsion; for dryer sheets, the nonwoven substrate and the applied softener coating, each sampled and tested as a distinct component, because the substrate and the coating have separate supply chains and separate metal profiles.   - **Ni substantiation:** Nickel is the analyte this category's exposure pathway is built around: nickel is a confirmed dermal sensitiser, and a residue deposited on fabric that is then worn is prolonged, repeated dermal contact. No sovereign sets a nickel maximum for a laundry or fabric-care product. Instruments checked: China GB 14930.1-2022 (the only sovereign detergent contaminant standard in the register; scope is Type A detergents used directly for food cleaning and Type B detergents for tableware and food-contact tools, equipment, packaging and containers, and it sets a total-arsenic ceiling only); China NMPA 2015 Safety and Technical Standards for Cosmetics Table 2 (lead, arsenic, mercury, cadmium); Korea MFDS Notice 2025-63 and its predecessor Notice 2024-9 (lead, cadmium, nickel, antimony, arsenic, mercury); Germany BVL 2017 technically-avoidable heavy metals in cosmetics (lead, cadmium, mercury, arsenic, antimony); Health Canada cosmetic heavy-metal impurity limits; the ASEAN Cosmetic Directive Annex (2019) and the Malaysia NPRA, Singapore HSA, Indonesia BPOM, Taiwan MoHW and New Zealand cosmetic group-standard equivalents; EAEU TR CU 009/2011; EU REACH Annex XVII entry 20 (organotin), entry 23 (cadmium in listed polymers and paints), entry 47 as amended by Regulation (EU) 301/2014 (Cr(VI) in skin-contact leather), entry 72 with Appendix 12 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 (lead, cadmium, arsenic and Cr(VI) in consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles) and entry 75 under Regulation (EU) 2020/2081 (tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures); EU Directive 2009/48/EC toy-safety migration limits; EU RoHS 2011/65/EU; and the dietary contaminant regimes (EU 2023/915, EU 2024/1987, Codex CXS 193, US FDA, FSANZ, Health Canada, UK, Hong Kong Cap. 132V), which are inapplicable both on scope and on basis. China NMPA 2015 Table 2 — the instrument that anchors lead, cadmium, arsenic and mercury on this row — sets no nickel limit at all, so for nickel the nearest applicable sovereign consumer-chemical value is the Korean statutory limit, which is the only regime in the register that sets one. The Korean instrument writes a product-form split (eye make-up 35, colour make-up 30, all other cosmetic products 10 mg/kg); the split is preserved and the "all other products" tier is the applicable one, because a fabric-care product is not a decorative make-up. A single coherent sovereign regime is read across per analyte rather than the lowest number picked across regimes, which would build a ceiling no sovereign enforces. Reading an injected-mixture ceiling across to a rinse-off or fabric-deposited topical product would conflate exposure routes, and its register basis is a tattoo/permanent-make-up mixture basis that this row's basis_guard excludes. Nickel is measured in soaps, detergents and household hygienic products in the routed corpus and is named for laundry care by greenseal2020-gs51-laundry-care-products, nydec2020-cleansing-product-disclosure-bmp and ecetoc1992-tr045-ni-co-cr-consumer-products, so a not-material determination is unavailable on the facts. **Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** No sovereign government sets a lead maximum for a laundry or fabric-care product. Instruments checked: China GB 14930.1-2022 (the only sovereign detergent contaminant standard in the register; scope is Type A detergents used directly for food cleaning and Type B detergents for tableware and food-contact tools, equipment, packaging and containers, and it sets a total-arsenic ceiling only); China NMPA 2015 Safety and Technical Standards for Cosmetics Table 2 (lead, arsenic, mercury, cadmium); Korea MFDS Notice 2025-63 and its predecessor Notice 2024-9 (lead, cadmium, nickel, antimony, arsenic, mercury); Germany BVL 2017 technically-avoidable heavy metals in cosmetics (lead, cadmium, mercury, arsenic, antimony); Health Canada cosmetic heavy-metal impurity limits; the ASEAN Cosmetic Directive Annex (2019) and the Malaysia NPRA, Singapore HSA, Indonesia BPOM, Taiwan MoHW and New Zealand cosmetic group-standard equivalents; EAEU TR CU 009/2011; EU REACH Annex XVII entry 20 (organotin), entry 23 (cadmium in listed polymers and paints), entry 47 as amended by Regulation (EU) 301/2014 (Cr(VI) in skin-contact leather), entry 72 with Appendix 12 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 (lead, cadmium, arsenic and Cr(VI) in consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles) and entry 75 under Regulation (EU) 2020/2081 (tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures); EU Directive 2009/48/EC toy-safety migration limits; EU RoHS 2011/65/EU; and the dietary contaminant regimes (EU 2023/915, EU 2024/1987, Codex CXS 193, US FDA, FSANZ, Health Canada, UK, Hong Kong Cap. 132V), which are inapplicable both on scope and on basis. China GB 14930.1-2022 does not reach this product form and carries no lead row in any event (its grouped heavy-metals line is expressed as combined heavy metals calculated as lead and is not loaded as a lead limit). The sector regimes — Green Seal GS-48 and GS-51 for laundry care products, the EPA Safer Choice programme and its Design for the Environment standard, the EU Ecolabel for detergents and cleaning products (Commission Decisions (EU) 2017/1214 to 2017/1219 and the 2024 user manual, with the JRC 2025 revision in progress), California SB 258 (the Cleaning Product Right to Know Act) and the New York DEC cleansing-product disclosure best-management practice — name lead as a substance of concern for laundry care but set no numeric ceiling, so they establish materiality and supply no number. A single coherent sovereign regime is read across per analyte rather than the lowest number picked across regimes, which would build a ceiling no sovereign enforces. China NMPA 2015 Table 2 is the strictest coherent STATUTORY consumer-chemical regime for lead, cadmium, total arsenic and total mercury (Korea MFDS is looser on lead at 20 and on total arsenic at 10 mg/kg; ASEAN, Health Canada and BVL are guidance), and it is already the read-across anchor of the live Category 12 (Household Cleaning and Dishwashing) standard for the identical logical move. Nickel is not regulated by the Chinese instrument at all, so for nickel the nearest applicable sovereign consumer-chemical value is the Korean statutory limit; that is the only regime in the register that sets one, and no number in this row was chosen by comparing outcomes. The Germany BVL 2017 values that the audit scan surfaces as stricter are orientation values for technically avoidable trace content, carried in the register with status 'guidance impurity limit' rather than as statutory maximum levels; Method v2.0 selects the strictest applicable MAXIMUM LEVEL set by a credible sovereign government, so a guidance orientation value is not selectable as an anchor. The same reasoning excludes the Health Canada cosmetic impurity limits and the ASEAN, Malaysia and Singapore guidance values. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 72 with Appendix 12 restricts lead, cadmium, total arsenic and hexavalent chromium to 1 mg/kg in consumer clothing, related accessories, textiles with skin contact similar to clothing, and footwear. That instrument governs the FABRIC and not the product applied to it, and it is expressed on a homogeneous-material-after-extraction basis rather than on the product-content basis of this category. It is disclosed here as a related but distinct scope and as the downstream constraint a certified product must not cause the treated textile to breach; it is never borrowed as this category's ceiling, and the row's basis_guard makes that borrowing impossible in the compiler. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 75 (Regulation (EU) 2020/2081) sets the strictest sovereign consumer-mixture metal limits in the register (lead 0.7, cadmium 0.5, total arsenic 0.5, mercury 0.5, Cr(VI) 0.5 and nickel 5 mg/kg), but its scope is mixtures for tattooing and permanent make-up, an intradermal-injection route. Reading an injected-mixture ceiling across to a rinse-off or fabric-deposited topical product would conflate exposure routes, and its register basis is a tattoo/permanent-make-up mixture basis that this row's basis_guard excludes. Lead is measured in this product class in the routed corpus, so a not-material determination is unavailable on the facts. Complete disposition of the stricter rows the compiler's audit safety-net surfaces for this row, none of which is adopted. Nickel is not regulated by the Chinese instrument at all, so for nickel the nearest applicable sovereign consumer-chemical value is the Korean statutory limit; that is the only regime in the register that sets one, and no number in this row was chosen by comparing outcomes. The Germany BVL 2017 values that the audit scan surfaces as stricter are orientation values for technically avoidable trace content, carried in the register with status 'guidance impurity limit' rather than as statutory maximum levels; Method v2.0 selects the strictest applicable MAXIMUM LEVEL set by a credible sovereign government, so a guidance orientation value is not selectable as an anchor. The same reasoning excludes the Health Canada cosmetic impurity limits and the ASEAN, Malaysia and Singapore guidance values — the Health Canada cosmetic cadmium value of 3 mg/kg that the audit scan surfaces is such a guidance impurity limit, and adopting it for cadmium while lead, arsenic and mercury come from the Chinese instrument would mix regimes for no principled reason. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 72 with Appendix 12 restricts lead, cadmium, total arsenic and hexavalent chromium to 1 mg/kg in consumer clothing, related accessories, textiles with skin contact similar to clothing, and footwear. That instrument governs the FABRIC and not the product applied to it, and it is expressed on a homogeneous-material-after-extraction basis rather than on the product-content basis of this category. It is disclosed here as a related but distinct scope and as the downstream constraint a certified product must not cause the treated textile to breach; it is never borrowed as this category's ceiling, and the row's basis_guard makes that borrowing impossible in the compiler. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 75 (Regulation (EU) 2020/2081) sets the strictest sovereign consumer-mixture metal limits in the register (lead 0.7, cadmium 0.5, total arsenic 0.5, mercury 0.5, Cr(VI) 0.5 and nickel 5 mg/kg), but its scope is mixtures for tattooing and permanent make-up, an intradermal-injection route. Reading an injected-mixture ceiling across to a rinse-off or fabric-deposited topical product would conflate exposure routes, and its register basis is a tattoo/permanent-make-up mixture basis that this row's basis_guard excludes. Cadmium is measured in this product class in the routed corpus, so a not-material determination is unavailable on the facts. Complete disposition of the stricter rows the compiler's audit safety-net surfaces for this row, none of which is adopted. (1) de_bvl2017_pb_cosmetics_general_2000 (2 mg/kg), de_bvl2017_cd_cosmetics_general_100 (0.1), de_bvl2017_tas_cosmetics_general_500 (0.5) and de_bvl2017_thg_cosmetics_general_100 (0.1): Germany BVL 2017 orientation values for technically avoidable trace content, register status 'guidance impurity limit', not statutory maximum levels, and therefore not selectable under Method v2.0. (2) hc_cosmetic_cd_3ppm (3 mg/kg): a Health Canada cosmetic impurity guidance limit, excluded for the same reason, and adopting it for cadmium alone would additionally mix regimes. (3) eu_2018_1513_pb_consumer_textiles_1mgkg, eu_2018_1513_cd_consumer_textiles_1mgkg and eu_2018_1513_tas_consumer_textiles_1mgkg (1 mg/kg each): the EU consumer-textile restriction, which governs the FABRIC and not the product applied to it, on a homogeneous-material-after-extraction basis rather than this row's product-content basis; carried as a downstream constraint and companion limit, and blocked from anchoring by the row's basis_guard. The audit_exclude_scope on this row removes only the oral/mucosal toothpaste tier, tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures, and colour-additive and colourant specifications; against the register as built it suppresses no row that the audit scope would otherwise surface, so nothing was excluded to make this list shorter. (4) Two further stricter rows exist that the compiler's audit safety-net structurally CANNOT surface, because it filters to the credible-regulator set, and they are therefore recorded by hand rather than left invisible. asean2019_th_cd_cosmetic_products_3000 (Thailand, cadmium 3 mg/kg) is a guidance impurity limit and falls to the same reasoning as the Health Canada value. eaeu_trcu009_2011_pb_natural_raw_material_cosmetics_5mgkg (EAEU, lead 5 mg/kg) is the only STATUTORY row anywhere in the consumer-chemical product-content basis family that is stricter than an anchor adopted here, and it is not adopted for three reasons: the EAEU is not in the HMTc credible-regulator set and admitting it is a governance ruling outside this derivation; its scope is formulation-conditioned (perfumery-cosmetic products containing more than 1 per cent natural plant or natural mineral raw material) rather than the general product tier, and that condition would be satisfied by some but not all rows in this category, which would fracture the category on a formulation test no sovereign wrote for laundry products; and the EAEU instrument does not dominate the adopted regime, since it is stricter on lead (5 against 10 mg/kg) but LOOSER on total arsenic (5 against 2 mg/kg), identical on mercury and silent on cadmium, so taking its lead value while keeping the Chinese arsenic value would compose a ceiling no sovereign enforces, which is precisely what the single-coherent-regime discipline exists to prevent. This enumeration is the output of a credible-set-blind sweep of every one of the 80 register rows the basis_guard admits, not of the automated scan alone, and it is complete as of this build. Verification method: total cadmium by microwave-assisted closed-vessel acid digestion (HNO3/H2O2, with a hydrofluoric-acid or lithium-metaborate fusion step where an aluminosilicate, silicate or clay mineral phase is present) followed by ICP-MS quantification against matrix-matched calibration standards, with a certified reference material and a reagent blank in every run. The sampling unit is one composite sample per production lot, drawn from the product as placed on the market, with component-level sampling of for liquids, the finished formulation; for sheets, the nonwoven substrate and the applied antistatic coating, each sampled and tested as a distinct component. A lot at or above the published ceiling fails; a lot inside the ceiling but outside the running per-row control band is escalated to ALARA review with raw-material trace-back.

  - **Ni substantiation:** Nickel is the analyte this category's exposure pathway is built around: nickel is a confirmed dermal sensitiser, and a residue deposited on fabric that is then worn is prolonged, repeated dermal contact. No sovereign sets a nickel maximum for a laundry or fabric-care product. Instruments checked: China GB 14930.1-2022 (the only sovereign detergent contaminant standard in the register; scope is Type A detergents used directly for food cleaning and Type B detergents for tableware and food-contact tools, equipment, packaging and containers, and it sets a total-arsenic ceiling only); China NMPA 2015 Safety and Technical Standards for Cosmetics Table 2 (lead, arsenic, mercury, cadmium); Korea MFDS Notice 2025-63 and its predecessor Notice 2024-9 (lead, cadmium, nickel, antimony, arsenic, mercury); Germany BVL 2017 technically-avoidable heavy metals in cosmetics (lead, cadmium, mercury, arsenic, antimony); Health Canada cosmetic heavy-metal impurity limits; the ASEAN Cosmetic Directive Annex (2019) and the Malaysia NPRA, Singapore HSA, Indonesia BPOM, Taiwan MoHW and New Zealand cosmetic group-standard equivalents; EAEU TR CU 009/2011; EU REACH Annex XVII entry 20 (organotin), entry 23 (cadmium in listed polymers and paints), entry 47 as amended by Regulation (EU) 301/2014 (Cr(VI) in skin-contact leather), entry 72 with Appendix 12 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 (lead, cadmium, arsenic and Cr(VI) in consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles) and entry 75 under Regulation (EU) 2020/2081 (tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures); EU Directive 2009/48/EC toy-safety migration limits; EU RoHS 2011/65/EU; and the dietary contaminant regimes (EU 2023/915, EU 2024/1987, Codex CXS 193, US FDA, FSANZ, Health Canada, UK, Hong Kong Cap. 132V), which are inapplicable both on scope and on basis. The Korean instrument writes a product-form split (eye make-up 35, colour make-up 30, all other cosmetic products 10 mg/kg); the split is preserved and the "all other products" tier is the applicable one, because a fabric-care product is not a decorative make-up. A single coherent sovereign regime is read across per analyte rather than the lowest number picked across regimes, which would build a ceiling no sovereign enforces. ### fabric-refresher-products — Fabric refresher products
- native basis: total heavy-metal content of the finished product as placed on the market (as-sold consumer-chemical product-content basis, mg/kg of product)
- cross-reference: A product whose primary function is scenting room air rather than treating fabric routes to Category 22 (Air Care and Fragrance-Release Products); a hard-surface odour remover routes to Category 12 (Household Cleaning and Dishwashing).

**Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** No sovereign government sets a lead maximum for a laundry or fabric-care product. Instruments checked: China GB 14930.1-2022 (the only sovereign detergent contaminant standard in the register; scope is Type A detergents used directly for food cleaning and Type B detergents for tableware and food-contact tools, equipment, packaging and containers, and it sets a total-arsenic ceiling only); China NMPA 2015 Safety and Technical Standards for Cosmetics Table 2 (lead, arsenic, mercury, cadmium); Korea MFDS Notice 2025-63 and its predecessor Notice 2024-9 (lead, cadmium, nickel, antimony, arsenic, mercury); Germany BVL 2017 technically-avoidable heavy metals in cosmetics (lead, cadmium, mercury, arsenic, antimony); Health Canada cosmetic heavy-metal impurity limits; the ASEAN Cosmetic Directive Annex (2019) and the Malaysia NPRA, Singapore HSA, Indonesia BPOM, Taiwan MoHW and New Zealand cosmetic group-standard equivalents; EAEU TR CU 009/2011; EU REACH Annex XVII entry 20 (organotin), entry 23 (cadmium in listed polymers and paints), entry 47 as amended by Regulation (EU) 301/2014 (Cr(VI) in skin-contact leather), entry 72 with Appendix 12 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 (lead, cadmium, arsenic and Cr(VI) in consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles) and entry 75 under Regulation (EU) 2020/2081 (tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures); EU Directive 2009/48/EC toy-safety migration limits; EU RoHS 2011/65/EU; and the dietary contaminant regimes (EU 2023/915, EU 2024/1987, Codex CXS 193, US FDA, FSANZ, Health Canada, UK, Hong Kong Cap. 132V), which are inapplicable both on scope and on basis. China GB 14930.1-2022 does not reach this product form and carries no lead row in any event (its grouped heavy-metals line is expressed as combined heavy metals calculated as lead and is not loaded as a lead limit). The sector regimes — Green Seal GS-48 and GS-51 for laundry care products, the EPA Safer Choice programme and its Design for the Environment standard, the EU Ecolabel for detergents and cleaning products (Commission Decisions (EU) 2017/1214 to 2017/1219 and the 2024 user manual, with the JRC 2025 revision in progress), California SB 258 (the Cleaning Product Right to Know Act) and the New York DEC cleansing-product disclosure best-management practice — name lead as a substance of concern for laundry care but set no numeric ceiling, so they establish materiality and supply no number. A single coherent sovereign regime is read across per analyte rather than the lowest number picked across regimes, which would build a ceiling no sovereign enforces. China NMPA 2015 Table 2 is the strictest coherent STATUTORY consumer-chemical regime for lead, cadmium, total arsenic and total mercury (Korea MFDS is looser on lead at 20 and on total arsenic at 10 mg/kg; ASEAN, Health Canada and BVL are guidance), and it is already the read-across anchor of the live Category 12 (Household Cleaning and Dishwashing) standard for the identical logical move. Nickel is not regulated by the Chinese instrument at all, so for nickel the nearest applicable sovereign consumer-chemical value is the Korean statutory limit; that is the only regime in the register that sets one, and no number in this row was chosen by comparing outcomes. The Germany BVL 2017 values that the audit scan surfaces as stricter are orientation values for technically avoidable trace content, carried in the register with status 'guidance impurity limit' rather than as statutory maximum levels; Method v2.0 selects the strictest applicable MAXIMUM LEVEL set by a credible sovereign government, so a guidance orientation value is not selectable as an anchor. The same reasoning excludes the Health Canada cosmetic impurity limits and the ASEAN, Malaysia and Singapore guidance values. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 72 with Appendix 12 restricts lead, cadmium, total arsenic and hexavalent chromium to 1 mg/kg in consumer clothing, related accessories, textiles with skin contact similar to clothing, and footwear. That instrument governs the FABRIC and not the product applied to it, and it is expressed on a homogeneous-material-after-extraction basis rather than on the product-content basis of this category. It is disclosed here as a related but distinct scope and as the downstream constraint a certified product must not cause the treated textile to breach; it is never borrowed as this category's ceiling, and the row's basis_guard makes that borrowing impossible in the compiler. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 75 (Regulation (EU) 2020/2081) sets the strictest sovereign consumer-mixture metal limits in the register (lead 0.7, cadmium 0.5, total arsenic 0.5, mercury 0.5, Cr(VI) 0.5 and nickel 5 mg/kg), but its scope is mixtures for tattooing and permanent make-up, an intradermal-injection route. Reading an injected-mixture ceiling across to a rinse-off or fabric-deposited topical product would conflate exposure routes, and its register basis is a tattoo/permanent-make-up mixture basis that this row's basis_guard excludes. Lead is measured in this product class in the routed corpus, so a not-material determination is unavailable on the facts. Complete disposition of the stricter rows the compiler's audit safety-net surfaces for this row, none of which is adopted. (1) de_bvl2017_pb_cosmetics_general_2000 (2 mg/kg), de_bvl2017_cd_cosmetics_general_100 (0.1), de_bvl2017_tas_cosmetics_general_500 (0.5) and de_bvl2017_thg_cosmetics_general_100 (0.1): Germany BVL 2017 orientation values for technically avoidable trace content, register status 'guidance impurity limit', not statutory maximum levels, and therefore not selectable under Method v2.0. (2) hc_cosmetic_cd_3ppm (3 mg/kg): a Health Canada cosmetic impurity guidance limit, excluded for the same reason, and adopting it for cadmium alone would additionally mix regimes. (3) eu_2018_1513_pb_consumer_textiles_1mgkg, eu_2018_1513_cd_consumer_textiles_1mgkg and eu_2018_1513_tas_consumer_textiles_1mgkg (1 mg/kg each): the EU consumer-textile restriction, which governs the FABRIC and not the product applied to it, on a homogeneous-material-after-extraction basis rather than this row's product-content basis; carried as a downstream constraint and companion limit, and blocked from anchoring by the row's basis_guard. The audit_exclude_scope on this row removes only the oral/mucosal toothpaste tier, tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures, and colour-additive and colourant specifications; against the register as built it suppresses no row that the audit scope would otherwise surface, so nothing was excluded to make this list shorter. (4) Two further stricter rows exist that the compiler's audit safety-net structurally CANNOT surface, because it filters to the credible-regulator set, and they are therefore recorded by hand rather than left invisible. asean2019_th_cd_cosmetic_products_3000 (Thailand, cadmium 3 mg/kg) is a guidance impurity limit and falls to the same reasoning as the Health Canada value. eaeu_trcu009_2011_pb_natural_raw_material_cosmetics_5mgkg (EAEU, lead 5 mg/kg) is the only STATUTORY row anywhere in the consumer-chemical product-content basis family that is stricter than an anchor adopted here, and it is not adopted for three reasons: the EAEU is not in the HMTc credible-regulator set and admitting it is a governance ruling outside this derivation; its scope is formulation-conditioned (perfumery-cosmetic products containing more than 1 per cent natural plant or natural mineral raw material) rather than the general product tier, and that condition would be satisfied by some but not all rows in this category, which would fracture the category on a formulation test no sovereign wrote for laundry products; and the EAEU instrument does not dominate the adopted regime, since it is stricter on lead (5 against 10 mg/kg) but LOOSER on total arsenic (5 against 2 mg/kg), identical on mercury and silent on cadmium, so taking its lead value while keeping the Chinese arsenic value would compose a ceiling no sovereign enforces, which is precisely what the single-coherent-regime discipline exists to prevent. This enumeration is the output of a credible-set-blind sweep of every one of the 80 register rows the basis_guard admits, not of the automated scan alone, and it is complete as of this build. Verification method: total lead by microwave-assisted closed-vessel acid digestion (HNO3/H2O2, with a hydrofluoric-acid or lithium-metaborate fusion step where an aluminosilicate, silicate or clay mineral phase is present) followed by ICP-MS quantification against matrix-matched calibration standards, with a certified reference material and a reagent blank in every run. The sampling unit is one composite sample per production lot, drawn from the product as placed on the market, with component-level sampling of the liquid concentrate and, for aerosol formats, the propellant-charged formulation together with the valve and actuator hardware in the product-contact path, each sampled and tested as a distinct component. A lot at or above the published ceiling fails; a lot inside the ceiling but outside the running per-row control band is escalated to ALARA review with raw-material trace-back.

  - ?? **Pb stricter-unselected (audit):** 1000 µg/kg [EU] eu_2018_1513_pb_consumer_textiles_1mgkg — "consumer clothing, related accessories, textiles"

A food-contact detergent limit does not automatically reach a fabric-care product, and nothing turns on the exclusion in any case because both GB 14930.1 values are LOOSER than the 2 mg/kg adopted here. It is disclosed here as a related but distinct scope and as the downstream constraint a certified product must not cause the treated textile to breach; it is never borrowed as this category's ceiling, and the row's basis_guard makes that borrowing impossible in the compiler. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 75 (Regulation (EU) 2020/2081) sets the strictest sovereign consumer-mixture metal limits in the register (lead 0.7, cadmium 0.5, total arsenic 0.5, mercury 0.5, Cr(VI) 0.5 and nickel 5 mg/kg), but its scope is mixtures for tattooing and permanent make-up, an intradermal-injection route. Reading an injected-mixture ceiling across to a rinse-off or fabric-deposited topical product would conflate exposure routes, and its register basis is a tattoo/permanent-make-up mixture basis that this row's basis_guard excludes. Arsenic is named for this product class by the sector regimes and measured in household hygienic and cleaning products in the routed corpus, so a not-material determination is unavailable on the facts. China NMPA 2015 Table 2 is the strictest coherent STATUTORY consumer-chemical regime for lead, cadmium, total arsenic and total mercury (Korea MFDS is looser on lead at 20 and on total arsenic at 10 mg/kg; ASEAN, Health Canada and BVL are guidance), and it is already the read-across anchor of the live Category 12 (Household Cleaning and Dishwashing) standard for the identical logical move. Nickel is not regulated by the Chinese instrument at all, so for nickel the nearest applicable sovereign consumer-chemical value is the Korean statutory limit; that is the only regime in the register that sets one, and no number in this row was chosen by comparing outcomes. The Germany BVL 2017 values that the audit scan surfaces as stricter are orientation values for technically avoidable trace content, carried in the register with status 'guidance impurity limit' rather than as statutory maximum levels; Method v2.0 selects the strictest applicable MAXIMUM LEVEL set by a credible sovereign government, so a guidance orientation value is not selectable as an anchor. The same reasoning excludes the Health Canada cosmetic impurity limits and the ASEAN, Malaysia and Singapore guidance values. Deliberate-use mercury allowances for eye-area preservatives (India, New Zealand, South Africa) are not contaminant limits and are not read across. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 75 (Regulation (EU) 2020/2081) sets the strictest sovereign consumer-mixture metal limits in the register (lead 0.7, cadmium 0.5, total arsenic 0.5, mercury 0.5, Cr(VI) 0.5 and nickel 5 mg/kg), but its scope is mixtures for tattooing and permanent make-up, an intradermal-injection route. Reading an injected-mixture ceiling across to a rinse-off or fabric-deposited topical product would conflate exposure routes, and its register basis is a tattoo/permanent-make-up mixture basis that this row's basis_guard excludes. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 72 Appendix 12 sets no mercury row for consumer textiles, so no downstream fabric ceiling is available for this analyte. Mercury is named for this product class by greenseal2020-gs51-laundry-care-products, nydec2020-cleansing-product-disclosure-bmp and epa2024-safer-choice-standard-dfe, so a not-material determination is unavailable on the facts. Complete disposition of the stricter rows the compiler's audit safety-net surfaces for this row, none of which is adopted. (1) de_bvl2017_pb_cosmetics_general_2000 (2 mg/kg), de_bvl2017_cd_cosmetics_general_100 (0.1), de_bvl2017_tas_cosmetics_general_500 (0.5) and de_bvl2017_thg_cosmetics_general_100 (0.1): Germany BVL 2017 orientation values for technically avoidable trace content, register status 'guidance impurity limit', not statutory maximum levels, and therefore not selectable under Method v2.0. (2) hc_cosmetic_cd_3ppm (3 mg/kg): a Health Canada cosmetic impurity guidance limit, excluded for the same reason, and adopting it for cadmium alone would additionally mix regimes. (3) eu_2018_1513_pb_consumer_textiles_1mgkg, eu_2018_1513_cd_consumer_textiles_1mgkg and eu_2018_1513_tas_consumer_textiles_1mgkg (1 mg/kg each): the EU consumer-textile restriction, which governs the FABRIC and not the product applied to it, on a homogeneous-material-after-extraction basis rather than this row's product-content basis; carried as a downstream constraint and companion limit, and blocked from anchoring by the row's basis_guard. The audit_exclude_scope on this row removes only the oral/mucosal toothpaste tier, tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures, and colour-additive and colourant specifications; against the register as built it suppresses no row that the audit scope would otherwise surface, so nothing was excluded to make this list shorter. (4) Two further stricter rows exist that the compiler's audit safety-net structurally CANNOT surface, because it filters to the credible-regulator set, and they are therefore recorded by hand rather than left invisible. asean2019_th_cd_cosmetic_products_3000 (Thailand, cadmium 3 mg/kg) is a guidance impurity limit and falls to the same reasoning as the Health Canada value. eaeu_trcu009_2011_pb_natural_raw_material_cosmetics_5mgkg (EAEU, lead 5 mg/kg) is the only STATUTORY row anywhere in the consumer-chemical product-content basis family that is stricter than an anchor adopted here, and it is not adopted for three reasons: the EAEU is not in the HMTc credible-regulator set and admitting it is a governance ruling outside this derivation; its scope is formulation-conditioned (perfumery-cosmetic products containing more than 1 per cent natural plant or natural mineral raw material) rather than the general product tier, and that condition would be satisfied by some but not all rows in this category, which would fracture the category on a formulation test no sovereign wrote for laundry products; and the EAEU instrument does not dominate the adopted regime, since it is stricter on lead (5 against 10 mg/kg) but LOOSER on total arsenic (5 against 2 mg/kg), identical on mercury and silent on cadmium, so taking its lead value while keeping the Chinese arsenic value would compose a ceiling no sovereign enforces, which is precisely what the single-coherent-regime discipline exists to prevent. This enumeration is the output of a credible-set-blind sweep of every one of the 80 register rows the basis_guard admits, not of the automated scan alone, and it is complete as of this build. Verification method: total total mercury by microwave-assisted closed-vessel acid digestion (HNO3/H2O2, with a hydrofluoric-acid or lithium-metaborate fusion step where an aluminosilicate, silicate or clay mineral phase is present) followed by ICP-MS quantification against matrix-matched calibration standards, with a certified reference material and a reagent blank in every run. The sampling unit is one composite sample per production lot, drawn from the product as placed on the market, with component-level sampling of the liquid concentrate and, for aerosol formats, the propellant-charged formulation together with the valve and actuator hardware in the product-contact path, each sampled and tested as a distinct component. A lot at or above the published ceiling fails; a lot inside the ceiling but outside the running per-row control band is escalated to ALARA review with raw-material trace-back.

  - **Ni substantiation:** Nickel is the analyte this category's exposure pathway is built around: nickel is a confirmed dermal sensitiser, and a residue deposited on fabric that is then worn is prolonged, repeated dermal contact. No sovereign sets a nickel maximum for a laundry or fabric-care product. Instruments checked: China GB 14930.1-2022 (the only sovereign detergent contaminant standard in the register; scope is Type A detergents used directly for food cleaning and Type B detergents for tableware and food-contact tools, equipment, packaging and containers, and it sets a total-arsenic ceiling only); China NMPA 2015 Safety and Technical Standards for Cosmetics Table 2 (lead, arsenic, mercury, cadmium); Korea MFDS Notice 2025-63 and its predecessor Notice 2024-9 (lead, cadmium, nickel, antimony, arsenic, mercury); Germany BVL 2017 technically-avoidable heavy metals in cosmetics (lead, cadmium, mercury, arsenic, antimony); Health Canada cosmetic heavy-metal impurity limits; the ASEAN Cosmetic Directive Annex (2019) and the Malaysia NPRA, Singapore HSA, Indonesia BPOM, Taiwan MoHW and New Zealand cosmetic group-standard equivalents; EAEU TR CU 009/2011; EU REACH Annex XVII entry 20 (organotin), entry 23 (cadmium in listed polymers and paints), entry 47 as amended by Regulation (EU) 301/2014 (Cr(VI) in skin-contact leather), entry 72 with Appendix 12 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 (lead, cadmium, arsenic and Cr(VI) in consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles) and entry 75 under Regulation (EU) 2020/2081 (tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures); EU Directive 2009/48/EC toy-safety migration limits; EU RoHS 2011/65/EU; and the dietary contaminant regimes (EU 2023/915, EU 2024/1987, Codex CXS 193, US FDA, FSANZ, Health Canada, UK, Hong Kong Cap. 132V), which are inapplicable both on scope and on basis. China NMPA 2015 Table 2 — the instrument that anchors lead, cadmium, arsenic and mercury on this row — sets no nickel limit at all, so for nickel the nearest applicable sovereign consumer-chemical value is the Korean statutory limit, which is the only regime in the register that sets one. The Korean instrument writes a product-form split (eye make-up 35, colour make-up 30, all other cosmetic products 10 mg/kg); the split is preserved and the "all other products" tier is the applicable one, because a fabric-care product is not a decorative make-up. A single coherent sovereign regime is read across per analyte rather than the lowest number picked across regimes, which would build a ceiling no sovereign enforces. **Audit / substantiation notes**
  - **Pb substantiation:** No sovereign government sets a lead maximum for a laundry or fabric-care product. Instruments checked: China GB 14930.1-2022 (the only sovereign detergent contaminant standard in the register; scope is Type A detergents used directly for food cleaning and Type B detergents for tableware and food-contact tools, equipment, packaging and containers, and it sets a total-arsenic ceiling only); China NMPA 2015 Safety and Technical Standards for Cosmetics Table 2 (lead, arsenic, mercury, cadmium); Korea MFDS Notice 2025-63 and its predecessor Notice 2024-9 (lead, cadmium, nickel, antimony, arsenic, mercury); Germany BVL 2017 technically-avoidable heavy metals in cosmetics (lead, cadmium, mercury, arsenic, antimony); Health Canada cosmetic heavy-metal impurity limits; the ASEAN Cosmetic Directive Annex (2019) and the Malaysia NPRA, Singapore HSA, Indonesia BPOM, Taiwan MoHW and New Zealand cosmetic group-standard equivalents; EAEU TR CU 009/2011; EU REACH Annex XVII entry 20 (organotin), entry 23 (cadmium in listed polymers and paints), entry 47 as amended by Regulation (EU) 301/2014 (Cr(VI) in skin-contact leather), entry 72 with Appendix 12 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 (lead, cadmium, arsenic and Cr(VI) in consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles) and entry 75 under Regulation (EU) 2020/2081 (tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures); EU Directive 2009/48/EC toy-safety migration limits; EU RoHS 2011/65/EU; and the dietary contaminant regimes (EU 2023/915, EU 2024/1987, Codex CXS 193, US FDA, FSANZ, Health Canada, UK, Hong Kong Cap. 132V), which are inapplicable both on scope and on basis. China GB 14930.1-2022 does not reach this product form and carries no lead row in any event (its grouped heavy-metals line is expressed as combined heavy metals calculated as lead and is not loaded as a lead limit). The sector regimes — Green Seal GS-48 and GS-51 for laundry care products, the EPA Safer Choice programme and its Design for the Environment standard, the EU Ecolabel for detergents and cleaning products (Commission Decisions (EU) 2017/1214 to 2017/1219 and the 2024 user manual, with the JRC 2025 revision in progress), California SB 258 (the Cleaning Product Right to Know Act) and the New York DEC cleansing-product disclosure best-management practice — name lead as a substance of concern for laundry care but set no numeric ceiling, so they establish materiality and supply no number. A single coherent sovereign regime is read across per analyte rather than the lowest number picked across regimes, which would build a ceiling no sovereign enforces. China NMPA 2015 Table 2 is the strictest coherent STATUTORY consumer-chemical regime for lead, cadmium, total arsenic and total mercury (Korea MFDS is looser on lead at 20 and on total arsenic at 10 mg/kg; ASEAN, Health Canada and BVL are guidance), and it is already the read-across anchor of the live Category 12 (Household Cleaning and Dishwashing) standard for the identical logical move. Nickel is not regulated by the Chinese instrument at all, so for nickel the nearest applicable sovereign consumer-chemical value is the Korean statutory limit; that is the only regime in the register that sets one, and no number in this row was chosen by comparing outcomes. The Germany BVL 2017 values that the audit scan surfaces as stricter are orientation values for technically avoidable trace content, carried in the register with status 'guidance impurity limit' rather than as statutory maximum levels; Method v2.0 selects the strictest applicable MAXIMUM LEVEL set by a credible sovereign government, so a guidance orientation value is not selectable as an anchor. The same reasoning excludes the Health Canada cosmetic impurity limits and the ASEAN, Malaysia and Singapore guidance values. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 72 with Appendix 12 restricts lead, cadmium, total arsenic and hexavalent chromium to 1 mg/kg in consumer clothing, related accessories, textiles with skin contact similar to clothing, and footwear. That instrument governs the FABRIC and not the product applied to it, and it is expressed on a homogeneous-material-after-extraction basis rather than on the product-content basis of this category. It is disclosed here as a related but distinct scope and as the downstream constraint a certified product must not cause the treated textile to breach; it is never borrowed as this category's ceiling, and the row's basis_guard makes that borrowing impossible in the compiler. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 75 (Regulation (EU) 2020/2081) sets the strictest sovereign consumer-mixture metal limits in the register (lead 0.7, cadmium 0.5, total arsenic 0.5, mercury 0.5, Cr(VI) 0.5 and nickel 5 mg/kg), but its scope is mixtures for tattooing and permanent make-up, an intradermal-injection route. Reading an injected-mixture ceiling across to a rinse-off or fabric-deposited topical product would conflate exposure routes, and its register basis is a tattoo/permanent-make-up mixture basis that this row's basis_guard excludes. Lead is measured in this product class in the routed corpus, so a not-material determination is unavailable on the facts. Complete disposition of the stricter rows the compiler's audit safety-net surfaces for this row, none of which is adopted. (1) de_bvl2017_pb_cosmetics_general_2000 (2 mg/kg), de_bvl2017_cd_cosmetics_general_100 (0.1), de_bvl2017_tas_cosmetics_general_500 (0.5) and de_bvl2017_thg_cosmetics_general_100 (0.1): Germany BVL 2017 orientation values for technically avoidable trace content, register status 'guidance impurity limit', not statutory maximum levels, and therefore not selectable under Method v2.0. (2) hc_cosmetic_cd_3ppm (3 mg/kg): a Health Canada cosmetic impurity guidance limit, excluded for the same reason, and adopting it for cadmium alone would additionally mix regimes. (3) eu_2018_1513_pb_consumer_textiles_1mgkg, eu_2018_1513_cd_consumer_textiles_1mgkg and eu_2018_1513_tas_consumer_textiles_1mgkg (1 mg/kg each): the EU consumer-textile restriction, which governs the FABRIC and not the product applied to it, on a homogeneous-material-after-extraction basis rather than this row's product-content basis; carried as a downstream constraint and companion limit, and blocked from anchoring by the row's basis_guard. (4) Two further stricter rows exist that the compiler's audit safety-net structurally CANNOT surface, because it filters to the credible-regulator set, and they are therefore recorded by hand rather than left invisible. asean2019_th_cd_cosmetic_products_3000 (Thailand, cadmium 3 mg/kg) is a guidance impurity limit and falls to the same reasoning as the Health Canada value. eaeu_trcu009_2011_pb_natural_raw_material_cosmetics_5mgkg (EAEU, lead 5 mg/kg) is the only STATUTORY row anywhere in the consumer-chemical product-content basis family that is stricter than an anchor adopted here, and it is not adopted for three reasons: the EAEU is not in the HMTc credible-regulator set and admitting it is a governance ruling outside this derivation; its scope is formulation-conditioned (perfumery-cosmetic products containing more than 1 per cent natural plant or natural mineral raw material) rather than the general product tier, and that condition would be satisfied by some but not all rows in this category, which would fracture the category on a formulation test no sovereign wrote for laundry products; and the EAEU instrument does not dominate the adopted regime, since it is stricter on lead (5 against 10 mg/kg) but LOOSER on total arsenic (5 against 2 mg/kg), identical on mercury and silent on cadmium, so taking its lead value while keeping the Chinese arsenic value would compose a ceiling no sovereign enforces, which is precisely what the single-coherent-regime discipline exists to prevent. This enumeration is the output of a credible-set-blind sweep of every one of the 80 register rows the basis_guard admits, not of the automated scan alone, and it is complete as of this build. Verification method: total cadmium by microwave-assisted closed-vessel acid digestion (HNO3/H2O2, with a hydrofluoric-acid or lithium-metaborate fusion step where an aluminosilicate, silicate or clay mineral phase is present) followed by ICP-MS quantification against matrix-matched calibration standards, with a certified reference material and a reagent blank in every run. The sampling unit is one composite sample per production lot, drawn from the product as placed on the market, with component-level sampling of the liquid concentrate and, for aerosol formats, the propellant-charged formulation together with the valve and actuator hardware in the product-contact path. A lot at or above the published ceiling fails; a lot inside the ceiling but outside the running per-row control band is escalated to ALARA review with raw-material trace-back.

  - **tAs substantiation:** No sovereign government sets a total-arsenic maximum for a laundry or fabric-care product. Instruments checked: China GB 14930.1-2022 (the only sovereign detergent contaminant standard in the register; scope is Type A detergents used directly for food cleaning and Type B detergents for tableware and food-contact tools, equipment, packaging and containers, and it sets a total-arsenic ceiling only); China NMPA 2015 Safety and Technical Standards for Cosmetics Table 2 (lead, arsenic, mercury, cadmium); Korea MFDS Notice 2025-63 and its predecessor Notice 2024-9 (lead, cadmium, nickel, antimony, arsenic, mercury); Germany BVL 2017 technically-avoidable heavy metals in cosmetics (lead, cadmium, mercury, arsenic, antimony); Health Canada cosmetic heavy-metal impurity limits; the ASEAN Cosmetic Directive Annex (2019) and the Malaysia NPRA, Singapore HSA, Indonesia BPOM, Taiwan MoHW and New Zealand cosmetic group-standard equivalents; EAEU TR CU 009/2011; EU REACH Annex XVII entry 20 (organotin), entry 23 (cadmium in listed polymers and paints), entry 47 as amended by Regulation (EU) 301/2014 (Cr(VI) in skin-contact leather), entry 72 with Appendix 12 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 (lead, cadmium, arsenic and Cr(VI) in consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles) and entry 75 under Regulation (EU) 2020/2081 (tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures); EU Directive 2009/48/EC toy-safety migration limits; EU RoHS 2011/65/EU; and the dietary contaminant regimes (EU 2023/915, EU 2024/1987, Codex CXS 193, US FDA, FSANZ, Health Canada, UK, Hong Kong Cap. 132V), which are inapplicable both on scope and on basis. China GB 14930.1-2022 is the one sovereign instrument that sets a detergent arsenic ceiling, and it is checked and excluded on scope: it governs Type A detergents used directly for food cleaning (3.0 mg/kg total arsenic) and Type B detergents for tableware and food-contact tools, equipment, packaging and containers (5.0 mg/kg), and a laundry detergent is neither, still less a fabric softener. A food-contact detergent limit does not automatically reach a fabric-care product, and nothing turns on the exclusion in any case because both GB 14930.1 values are LOOSER than the 2 mg/kg adopted here. A single coherent sovereign regime is read across per analyte rather than the lowest number picked across regimes, which would build a ceiling no sovereign enforces. China NMPA 2015 Table 2 is the strictest coherent STATUTORY consumer-chemical regime for lead, cadmium, total arsenic and total mercury (Korea MFDS is looser on lead at 20 and on total arsenic at 10 mg/kg; ASEAN, Health Canada and BVL are guidance), and it is already the read-across anchor of the live Category 12 (Household Cleaning and Dishwashing) standard for the identical logical move. Nickel is not regulated by the Chinese instrument at all, so for nickel the nearest applicable sovereign consumer-chemical value is the Korean statutory limit; that is the only regime in the register that sets one, and no number in this row was chosen by comparing outcomes. The Germany BVL 2017 values that the audit scan surfaces as stricter are orientation values for technically avoidable trace content, carried in the register with status 'guidance impurity limit' rather than as statutory maximum levels; Method v2.0 selects the strictest applicable MAXIMUM LEVEL set by a credible sovereign government, so a guidance orientation value is not selectable as an anchor. The same reasoning excludes the Health Canada cosmetic impurity limits and the ASEAN, Malaysia and Singapore guidance values. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 72 with Appendix 12 restricts lead, cadmium, total arsenic and hexavalent chromium to 1 mg/kg in consumer clothing, related accessories, textiles with skin contact similar to clothing, and footwear. That instrument governs the FABRIC and not the product applied to it, and it is expressed on a homogeneous-material-after-extraction basis rather than on the product-content basis of this category. It is disclosed here as a related but distinct scope and as the downstream constraint a certified product must not cause the treated textile to breach; it is never borrowed as this category's ceiling, and the row's basis_guard makes that borrowing impossible in the compiler. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 75 (Regulation (EU) 2020/2081) sets the strictest sovereign consumer-mixture metal limits in the register (lead 0.7, cadmium 0.5, total arsenic 0.5, mercury 0.5, Cr(VI) 0.5 and nickel 5 mg/kg), but its scope is mixtures for tattooing and permanent make-up, an intradermal-injection route. Reading an injected-mixture ceiling across to a rinse-off or fabric-deposited topical product would conflate exposure routes, and its register basis is a tattoo/permanent-make-up mixture basis that this row's basis_guard excludes. Arsenic is named for this product class by the sector regimes and measured in household hygienic and cleaning products in the routed corpus, so a not-material determination is unavailable on the facts. Complete disposition of the stricter rows the compiler's audit safety-net surfaces for this row, none of which is adopted. (2) hc_cosmetic_cd_3ppm (3 mg/kg): a Health Canada cosmetic impurity guidance limit, excluded for the same reason, and adopting it for cadmium alone would additionally mix regimes.   - **Ni substantiation:** Nickel is the analyte this category's exposure pathway is built around: nickel is a confirmed dermal sensitiser, and a residue deposited on fabric that is then worn is prolonged, repeated dermal contact. No sovereign sets a nickel maximum for a laundry or fabric-care product. Instruments checked: China GB 14930.1-2022 (the only sovereign detergent contaminant standard in the register; scope is Type A detergents used directly for food cleaning and Type B detergents for tableware and food-contact tools, equipment, packaging and containers, and it sets a total-arsenic ceiling only); China NMPA 2015 Safety and Technical Standards for Cosmetics Table 2 (lead, arsenic, mercury, cadmium); Korea MFDS Notice 2025-63 and its predecessor Notice 2024-9 (lead, cadmium, nickel, antimony, arsenic, mercury); Germany BVL 2017 technically-avoidable heavy metals in cosmetics (lead, cadmium, mercury, arsenic, antimony); Health Canada cosmetic heavy-metal impurity limits; the ASEAN Cosmetic Directive Annex (2019) and the Malaysia NPRA, Singapore HSA, Indonesia BPOM, Taiwan MoHW and New Zealand cosmetic group-standard equivalents; EAEU TR CU 009/2011; EU REACH Annex XVII entry 20 (organotin), entry 23 (cadmium in listed polymers and paints), entry 47 as amended by Regulation (EU) 301/2014 (Cr(VI) in skin-contact leather), entry 72 with Appendix 12 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 (lead, cadmium, arsenic and Cr(VI) in consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles) and entry 75 under Regulation (EU) 2020/2081 (tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures); EU Directive 2009/48/EC toy-safety migration limits; EU RoHS 2011/65/EU; and the dietary contaminant regimes (EU 2023/915, EU 2024/1987, Codex CXS 193, US FDA, FSANZ, Health Canada, UK, Hong Kong Cap. 132V), which are inapplicable both on scope and on basis. China NMPA 2015 Table 2 — the instrument that anchors lead, cadmium, arsenic and mercury on this row — sets no nickel limit at all, so for nickel the nearest applicable sovereign consumer-chemical value is the Korean statutory limit, which is the only regime in the register that sets one. The Korean instrument writes a product-form split (eye make-up 35, colour make-up 30, all other cosmetic products 10 mg/kg); the split is preserved and the "all other products" tier is the applicable one, because a fabric-care product is not a decorative make-up. A single coherent sovereign regime is read across per analyte rather than the lowest number picked across regimes, which would build a ceiling no sovereign enforces. China NMPA 2015 Table 2 is the strictest coherent STATUTORY consumer-chemical regime for lead, cadmium, total arsenic and total mercury (Korea MFDS is looser on lead at 20 and on total arsenic at 10 mg/kg; ASEAN, Health Canada and BVL are guidance), and it is already the read-across anchor of the live Category 12 (Household Cleaning and Dishwashing) standard for the identical logical move. Nickel is not regulated by the Chinese instrument at all, so for nickel the nearest applicable sovereign consumer-chemical value is the Korean statutory limit; that is the only regime in the register that sets one, and no number in this row was chosen by comparing outcomes. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 27 restricts nickel RELEASE from articles in prolonged skin contact rather than nickel content in a mixture and is not carried in the register as a content limit, so it is not read across; EU REACH Annex XVII entry 72 Appendix 12 sets no nickel row for consumer textiles, so no downstream fabric content ceiling is available for this analyte. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 75 (Regulation (EU) 2020/2081) sets the strictest sovereign consumer-mixture metal limits in the register (lead 0.7, cadmium 0.5, total arsenic 0.5, mercury 0.5, Cr(VI) 0.5 and nickel 5 mg/kg), but its scope is mixtures for tattooing and permanent make-up, an intradermal-injection route. Reading an injected-mixture ceiling across to a rinse-off or fabric-deposited topical product would conflate exposure routes, and its register basis is a tattoo/permanent-make-up mixture basis that this row's basis_guard excludes. Nickel is measured in soaps, detergents and household hygienic products in the routed corpus and is named for laundry care by greenseal2020-gs51-laundry-care-products, nydec2020-cleansing-product-disclosure-bmp and ecetoc1992-tr045-ni-co-cr-consumer-products, so a not-material determination is unavailable on the facts. Complete disposition of the stricter rows the compiler's audit safety-net surfaces for this row, none of which is adopted. (2) hc_cosmetic_cd_3ppm (3 mg/kg): a Health Canada cosmetic impurity guidance limit, excluded for the same reason, and adopting it for cadmium alone would additionally mix regimes. (3) eu_2018_1513_pb_consumer_textiles_1mgkg, eu_2018_1513_cd_consumer_textiles_1mgkg and eu_2018_1513_tas_consumer_textiles_1mgkg (1 mg/kg each): the EU consumer-textile restriction, which governs the FABRIC and not the product applied to it, on a homogeneous-material-after-extraction basis rather than this row's product-content basis; carried as a downstream constraint and companion limit, and blocked from anchoring by the row's basis_guard.
