Nickel is not regulated by the Chinese instrument at all, so for nickel the nearest applicable sovereign consumer-chemical value is the Korean statutory limit; that is the only regime in the register that sets one, and no number in this row was chosen by comparing outcomes. The Germany BVL 2017 values that the audit scan surfaces as stricter are orientation values for technically avoidable trace content, carried in the register with status 'guidance impurity limit' rather than as statutory maximum levels; Method v2.0 selects the strictest applicable MAXIMUM LEVEL set by a credible sovereign government, so a guidance orientation value is not selectable as an anchor. The same reasoning excludes the Health Canada cosmetic impurity limits and the ASEAN, Malaysia and Singapore guidance values. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 72 with Appendix 12 restricts lead, cadmium, total arsenic and hexavalent chromium to 1 mg/kg in consumer clothing, related accessories, textiles with skin contact similar to clothing, and footwear. That instrument governs the FABRIC and not the product applied to it, and it is expressed on a homogeneous-material-after-extraction basis rather than on the product-content basis of this category. It is disclosed here as a related but distinct scope and as the downstream constraint a certified product must not cause the treated textile to breach; it is never borrowed as this category's ceiling, and the row's basis_guard makes that borrowing impossible in the compiler. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 75 (Regulation (EU) 2020/2081) sets the strictest sovereign consumer-mixture metal limits in the register (lead 0.7, cadmium 0.5, total arsenic 0.5, mercury 0.5, Cr(VI) 0.5 and nickel 5 mg/kg), but its scope is mixtures for tattooing and permanent make-up, an intradermal-injection route. Reading an injected-mixture ceiling across to a rinse-off or fabric-deposited topical product would conflate exposure routes, and its register basis is a tattoo/permanent-make-up mixture basis that this row's basis_guard excludes. Arsenic is named for this product class by the sector regimes and measured in household hygienic and cleaning products in the routed corpus, so a not-material determination is unavailable on the facts. Complete disposition of the stricter rows the compiler's audit safety-net surfaces for this row, none of which is adopted. (1) de_bvl2017_pb_cosmetics_general_2000 (2 mg/kg), de_bvl2017_cd_cosmetics_general_100 (0.1), de_bvl2017_tas_cosmetics_general_500 (0.5) and de_bvl2017_thg_cosmetics_general_100 (0.1): Germany BVL 2017 orientation values for technically avoidable trace content, register status 'guidance impurity limit', not statutory maximum levels, and therefore not selectable under Method v2.0. (2) hc_cosmetic_cd_3ppm (3 mg/kg): a Health Canada cosmetic impurity guidance limit, excluded for the same reason, and adopting it for cadmium alone would additionally mix regimes. (3) eu_2018_1513_pb_consumer_textiles_1mgkg, eu_2018_1513_cd_consumer_textiles_1mgkg and eu_2018_1513_tas_consumer_textiles_1mgkg (1 mg/kg each): the EU consumer-textile restriction, which governs the FABRIC and not the product applied to it, on a homogeneous-material-after-extraction basis rather than this row's product-content basis; carried as a downstream constraint and companion limit, and blocked from anchoring by the row's basis_guard. The audit_exclude_scope on this row removes only the oral/mucosal toothpaste tier, tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures, and colour-additive and colourant specifications; against the register as built it suppresses no row that the audit scope would otherwise surface, so nothing was excluded to make this list shorter. (4) Two further stricter rows exist that the compiler's audit safety-net structurally CANNOT surface, because it filters to the credible-regulator set, and they are therefore recorded by hand rather than left invisible. asean2019_th_cd_cosmetic_products_3000 (Thailand, cadmium 3 mg/kg) is a guidance impurity limit and falls to the same reasoning as the Health Canada value. eaeu_trcu009_2011_pb_natural_raw_material_cosmetics_5mgkg (EAEU, lead 5 mg/kg) is the only STATUTORY row anywhere in the consumer-chemical product-content basis family that is stricter than an anchor adopted here, and it is not adopted for three reasons: the EAEU is not in the HMTc credible-regulator set and admitting it is a governance ruling outside this derivation; its scope is formulation-conditioned (perfumery-cosmetic products containing more than 1 per cent natural plant or natural mineral raw material) rather than the general product tier, and that condition would be satisfied by some but not all rows in this category, which would fracture the category on a formulation test no sovereign wrote for laundry products; and the EAEU instrument does not dominate the adopted regime, since it is stricter on lead (5 against 10 mg/kg) but LOOSER on total arsenic (5 against 2 mg/kg), identical on mercury and silent on cadmium, so taking its lead value while keeping the Chinese arsenic value would compose a ceiling no sovereign enforces, which is precisely what the single-coherent-regime discipline exists to prevent. This enumeration is the output of a credible-set-blind sweep of every one of the 80 register rows the basis_guard admits, not of the automated scan alone, and it is complete as of this build. Verification method: total total arsenic by microwave-assisted closed-vessel acid digestion (HNO3/H2O2, with a hydrofluoric-acid or lithium-metaborate fusion step where an aluminosilicate, silicate or clay mineral phase is present, which is the normal case on this row) followed by ICP-MS quantification against matrix-matched calibration standards, with a certified reference material and a reagent blank in every run. The sampling unit is one composite sample per production lot, drawn from the product as placed on the market, with component-level sampling of each functional phase and each physical component separately — the wash-active phase, the softening or protective phase, any nonwoven substrate, and any film, coating or wrapper — each sampled and tested as a distinct component. A lot at or above the published ceiling fails; a lot inside the ceiling but outside the running per-row control band is escalated to ALARA review with raw-material trace-back.

  - **Ni substantiation:** Nickel is the analyte this category's exposure pathway is built around: nickel is a confirmed dermal sensitiser, and a residue deposited on fabric that is then worn is prolonged, repeated dermal contact. No sovereign sets a nickel maximum for a laundry or fabric-care product. Instruments checked: China GB 14930.1-2022 (the only sovereign detergent contaminant standard in the register; scope is Type A detergents used directly for food cleaning and Type B detergents for tableware and food-contact tools, equipment, packaging and containers, and it sets a total-arsenic ceiling only); China NMPA 2015 Safety and Technical Standards for Cosmetics Table 2 (lead, arsenic, mercury, cadmium); Korea MFDS Notice 2025-63 and its predecessor Notice 2024-9 (lead, cadmium, nickel, antimony, arsenic, mercury); Germany BVL 2017 technically-avoidable heavy metals in cosmetics (lead, cadmium, mercury, arsenic, antimony); Health Canada cosmetic heavy-metal impurity limits; the ASEAN Cosmetic Directive Annex (2019) and the Malaysia NPRA, Singapore HSA, Indonesia BPOM, Taiwan MoHW and New Zealand cosmetic group-standard equivalents; EAEU TR CU 009/2011; EU REACH Annex XVII entry 20 (organotin), entry 23 (cadmium in listed polymers and paints), entry 47 as amended by Regulation (EU) 301/2014 (Cr(VI) in skin-contact leather), entry 72 with Appendix 12 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 (lead, cadmium, arsenic and Cr(VI) in consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles) and entry 75 under Regulation (EU) 2020/2081 (tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures); EU Directive 2009/48/EC toy-safety migration limits; EU RoHS 2011/65/EU; and the dietary contaminant regimes (EU 2023/915, EU 2024/1987, Codex CXS 193, US FDA, FSANZ, Health Canada, UK, Hong Kong Cap. 132V), which are inapplicable both on scope and on basis. China NMPA 2015 Table 2 — the instrument that anchors lead, cadmium, arsenic and mercury on this row — sets no nickel limit at all, so for nickel the nearest applicable sovereign consumer-chemical value is the Korean statutory limit, which is the only regime in the register that sets one. The Korean instrument writes a product-form split (eye make-up 35, colour make-up 30, all other cosmetic products 10 mg/kg); the split is preserved and the "all other products" tier is the applicable one, because a fabric-care product is not a decorative make-up. A single coherent sovereign regime is read across per analyte rather than the lowest number picked across regimes, which would build a ceiling no sovereign enforces. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 75 (Regulation (EU) 2020/2081) sets the strictest sovereign consumer-mixture metal limits in the register (lead 0.7, cadmium 0.5, total arsenic 0.5, mercury 0.5, Cr(VI) 0.5 and nickel 5 mg/kg), but its scope is mixtures for tattooing and permanent make-up, an intradermal-injection route. Reading an injected-mixture ceiling across to a rinse-off or fabric-deposited topical product would conflate exposure routes, and its register basis is a tattoo/permanent-make-up mixture basis that this row's basis_guard excludes. Nickel is measured in soaps, detergents and household hygienic products in the routed corpus and is named for laundry care by greenseal2020-gs51-laundry-care-products, nydec2020-cleansing-product-disclosure-bmp and ecetoc1992-tr045-ni-co-cr-consumer-products, so a not-material determination is unavailable on the facts. ### fabric-contact-products-other — Other fabric-contact home products (case-by-case)
- native basis: total heavy-metal content of the finished product as placed on the market (as-sold consumer-chemical product-content basis, mg/kg of product)
- cross-reference: A product intended for hard surfaces rather than fabrics routes to Category 12 (Household Cleaning and Dishwashing); a product whose primary function is scenting room air routes to Category 22 (Air Care and Fragrance-Release Products); a finished textile article itself is not a Category 19 product and is governed as a textile.

(3) eu_2018_1513_pb_consumer_textiles_1mgkg, eu_2018_1513_cd_consumer_textiles_1mgkg and eu_2018_1513_tas_consumer_textiles_1mgkg (1 mg/kg each): the EU consumer-textile restriction, which governs the FABRIC and not the product applied to it, on a homogeneous-material-after-extraction basis rather than this row's product-content basis; carried as a downstream constraint and companion limit, and blocked from anchoring by the row's basis_guard. The audit_exclude_scope on this row removes only the oral/mucosal toothpaste tier, tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures, and colour-additive and colourant specifications; against the register as built it suppresses no row that the audit scope would otherwise surface, so nothing was excluded to make this list shorter. (4) Two further stricter rows exist that the compiler's audit safety-net structurally CANNOT surface, because it filters to the credible-regulator set, and they are therefore recorded by hand rather than left invisible. asean2019_th_cd_cosmetic_products_3000 (Thailand, cadmium 3 mg/kg) is a guidance impurity limit and falls to the same reasoning as the Health Canada value. eaeu_trcu009_2011_pb_natural_raw_material_cosmetics_5mgkg (EAEU, lead 5 mg/kg) is the only STATUTORY row anywhere in the consumer-chemical product-content basis family that is stricter than an anchor adopted here, and it is not adopted for three reasons: the EAEU is not in the HMTc credible-regulator set and admitting it is a governance ruling outside this derivation; its scope is formulation-conditioned (perfumery-cosmetic products containing more than 1 per cent natural plant or natural mineral raw material) rather than the general product tier, and that condition would be satisfied by some but not all rows in this category, which would fracture the category on a formulation test no sovereign wrote for laundry products; and the EAEU instrument does not dominate the adopted regime, since it is stricter on lead (5 against 10 mg/kg) but LOOSER on total arsenic (5 against 2 mg/kg), identical on mercury and silent on cadmium, so taking its lead value while keeping the Chinese arsenic value would compose a ceiling no sovereign enforces, which is precisely what the single-coherent-regime discipline exists to prevent. This enumeration is the output of a credible-set-blind sweep of every one of the 80 register rows the basis_guard admits, not of the automated scan alone, and it is complete as of this build. Verification method: total lead by microwave-assisted closed-vessel acid digestion (HNO3/H2O2, with a hydrofluoric-acid or lithium-metaborate fusion step where an aluminosilicate, silicate or clay mineral phase is present, which is the normal case on this row) followed by ICP-MS quantification against matrix-matched calibration standards, with a certified reference material and a reagent blank in every run. The sampling unit is one composite sample per production lot, drawn from the product as placed on the market, with component-level sampling of every physical component and functional phase of the article, enumerated in the applicant's Step 0 scope determination and each sampled and tested as a distinct component; solid and sheet formats are separated into substrate and applied coating. A lot at or above the published ceiling fails; a lot inside the ceiling but outside the running per-row control band is escalated to ALARA review with raw-material trace-back.

  - ?? **Pb stricter-unselected (audit):** 1000 µg/kg [EU] eu_2018_1513_pb_consumer_textiles_1mgkg — "consumer clothing, related accessories, textiles"
  - **Cd substantiation:** No sovereign government sets a cadmium maximum for a laundry or fabric-care product. Instruments checked: China GB 14930.1-2022 (the only sovereign detergent contaminant standard in the register; scope is Type A detergents used directly for food cleaning and Type B detergents for tableware and food-contact tools, equipment, packaging and containers, and it sets a total-arsenic ceiling only); China NMPA 2015 Safety and Technical Standards for Cosmetics Table 2 (lead, arsenic, mercury, cadmium); Korea MFDS Notice 2025-63 and its predecessor Notice 2024-9 (lead, cadmium, nickel, antimony, arsenic, mercury); Germany BVL 2017 technically-avoidable heavy metals in cosmetics (lead, cadmium, mercury, arsenic, antimony); Health Canada cosmetic heavy-metal impurity limits; the ASEAN Cosmetic Directive Annex (2019) and the Malaysia NPRA, Singapore HSA, Indonesia BPOM, Taiwan MoHW and New Zealand cosmetic group-standard equivalents; EAEU TR CU 009/2011; EU REACH Annex XVII entry 20 (organotin), entry 23 (cadmium in listed polymers and paints), entry 47 as amended by Regulation (EU) 301/2014 (Cr(VI) in skin-contact leather), entry 72 with Appendix 12 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 (lead, cadmium, arsenic and Cr(VI) in consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles) and entry 75 under Regulation (EU) 2020/2081 (tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures); EU Directive 2009/48/EC toy-safety migration limits; EU RoHS 2011/65/EU; and the dietary contaminant regimes (EU 2023/915, EU 2024/1987, Codex CXS 193, US FDA, FSANZ, Health Canada, UK, Hong Kong Cap. 132V), which are inapplicable both on scope and on basis. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 23 (as amended by Regulation (EU) 494/2011) restricts cadmium in mixtures and articles produced from listed synthetic organic polymers and in paints, but it is a deliberate-use restriction on cadmium as a pigment and stabiliser in named polymer systems rather than a contaminant maximum for a consumer chemical, and the register row does not enumerate the polymer list, so it is recorded as checked and not adopted rather than read across on an unverified scope. A single coherent sovereign regime is read across per analyte rather than the lowest number picked across regimes, which would build a ceiling no sovereign enforces. China NMPA 2015 Table 2 is the strictest coherent STATUTORY consumer-chemical regime for lead, cadmium, total arsenic and total mercury (Korea MFDS is looser on lead at 20 and on total arsenic at 10 mg/kg; ASEAN, Health Canada and BVL are guidance), and it is already the read-across anchor of the live Category 12 (Household Cleaning and Dishwashing) standard for the identical logical move. Nickel is not regulated by the Chinese instrument at all, so for nickel the nearest applicable sovereign consumer-chemical value is the Korean statutory limit; that is the only regime in the register that sets one, and no number in this row was chosen by comparing outcomes. The Germany BVL 2017 values that the audit scan surfaces as stricter are orientation values for technically avoidable trace content, carried in the register with status 'guidance impurity limit' rather than as statutory maximum levels; Method v2.0 selects the strictest applicable MAXIMUM LEVEL set by a credible sovereign government, so a guidance orientation value is not selectable as an anchor. The same reasoning excludes the Health Canada cosmetic impurity limits and the ASEAN, Malaysia and Singapore guidance values — the Health Canada cosmetic cadmium value of 3 mg/kg that the audit scan surfaces is such a guidance impurity limit, and adopting it for cadmium while lead, arsenic and mercury come from the Chinese instrument would mix regimes for no principled reason. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 72 with Appendix 12 restricts lead, cadmium, total arsenic and hexavalent chromium to 1 mg/kg in consumer clothing, related accessories, textiles with skin contact similar to clothing, and footwear. That instrument governs the FABRIC and not the product applied to it, and it is expressed on a homogeneous-material-after-extraction basis rather than on the product-content basis of this category. It is disclosed here as a related but distinct scope and as the downstream constraint a certified product must not cause the treated textile to breach; it is never borrowed as this category's ceiling, and the row's basis_guard makes that borrowing impossible in the compiler. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 75 (Regulation (EU) 2020/2081) sets the strictest sovereign consumer-mixture metal limits in the register (lead 0.7, cadmium 0.5, total arsenic 0.5, mercury 0.5, Cr(VI) 0.5 and nickel 5 mg/kg), but its scope is mixtures for tattooing and permanent make-up, an intradermal-injection route. Reading an injected-mixture ceiling across to a rinse-off or fabric-deposited topical product would conflate exposure routes, and its register basis is a tattoo/permanent-make-up mixture basis that this row's basis_guard excludes. Cadmium is measured in this product class in the routed corpus, so a not-material determination is unavailable on the facts. Complete disposition of the stricter rows the compiler's audit safety-net surfaces for this row, none of which is adopted. (1) de_bvl2017_pb_cosmetics_general_2000 (2 mg/kg), de_bvl2017_cd_cosmetics_general_100 (0.1), de_bvl2017_tas_cosmetics_general_500 (0.5) and de_bvl2017_thg_cosmetics_general_100 (0.1): Germany BVL 2017 orientation values for technically avoidable trace content, register status 'guidance impurity limit', not statutory maximum levels, and therefore not selectable under Method v2.0. (2) hc_cosmetic_cd_3ppm (3 mg/kg): a Health Canada cosmetic impurity guidance limit, excluded for the same reason, and adopting it for cadmium alone would additionally mix regimes. (3) eu_2018_1513_pb_consumer_textiles_1mgkg, eu_2018_1513_cd_consumer_textiles_1mgkg and eu_2018_1513_tas_consumer_textiles_1mgkg (1 mg/kg each): the EU consumer-textile restriction, which governs the FABRIC and not the product applied to it, on a homogeneous-material-after-extraction basis rather than this row's product-content basis; carried as a downstream constraint and companion limit, and blocked from anchoring by the row's basis_guard. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 72 Appendix 12 sets no mercury row for consumer textiles, so no downstream fabric ceiling is available for this analyte. Mercury is named for this product class by greenseal2020-gs51-laundry-care-products, nydec2020-cleansing-product-disclosure-bmp and epa2024-safer-choice-standard-dfe, so a not-material determination is unavailable on the facts.   - **Ni substantiation:** Nickel is the analyte this category's exposure pathway is built around: nickel is a confirmed dermal sensitiser, and a residue deposited on fabric that is then worn is prolonged, repeated dermal contact. No sovereign sets a nickel maximum for a laundry or fabric-care product. Instruments checked: China GB 14930.1-2022 (the only sovereign detergent contaminant standard in the register; scope is Type A detergents used directly for food cleaning and Type B detergents for tableware and food-contact tools, equipment, packaging and containers, and it sets a total-arsenic ceiling only); China NMPA 2015 Safety and Technical Standards for Cosmetics Table 2 (lead, arsenic, mercury, cadmium); Korea MFDS Notice 2025-63 and its predecessor Notice 2024-9 (lead, cadmium, nickel, antimony, arsenic, mercury); Germany BVL 2017 technically-avoidable heavy metals in cosmetics (lead, cadmium, mercury, arsenic, antimony); Health Canada cosmetic heavy-metal impurity limits; the ASEAN Cosmetic Directive Annex (2019) and the Malaysia NPRA, Singapore HSA, Indonesia BPOM, Taiwan MoHW and New Zealand cosmetic group-standard equivalents; EAEU TR CU 009/2011; EU REACH Annex XVII entry 20 (organotin), entry 23 (cadmium in listed polymers and paints), entry 47 as amended by Regulation (EU) 301/2014 (Cr(VI) in skin-contact leather), entry 72 with Appendix 12 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2018/1513 (lead, cadmium, arsenic and Cr(VI) in consumer clothing and skin-contact textiles) and entry 75 under Regulation (EU) 2020/2081 (tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures); EU Directive 2009/48/EC toy-safety migration limits; EU RoHS 2011/65/EU; and the dietary contaminant regimes (EU 2023/915, EU 2024/1987, Codex CXS 193, US FDA, FSANZ, Health Canada, UK, Hong Kong Cap. 132V), which are inapplicable both on scope and on basis. China NMPA 2015 Table 2 — the instrument that anchors lead, cadmium, arsenic and mercury on this row — sets no nickel limit at all, so for nickel the nearest applicable sovereign consumer-chemical value is the Korean statutory limit, which is the only regime in the register that sets one. The Korean instrument writes a product-form split (eye make-up 35, colour make-up 30, all other cosmetic products 10 mg/kg); the split is preserved and the "all other products" tier is the applicable one, because a fabric-care product is not a decorative make-up. A single coherent sovereign regime is read across per analyte rather than the lowest number picked across regimes, which would build a ceiling no sovereign enforces. China NMPA 2015 Table 2 is the strictest coherent STATUTORY consumer-chemical regime for lead, cadmium, total arsenic and total mercury (Korea MFDS is looser on lead at 20 and on total arsenic at 10 mg/kg; ASEAN, Health Canada and BVL are guidance), and it is already the read-across anchor of the live Category 12 (Household Cleaning and Dishwashing) standard for the identical logical move. Nickel is not regulated by the Chinese instrument at all, so for nickel the nearest applicable sovereign consumer-chemical value is the Korean statutory limit; that is the only regime in the register that sets one, and no number in this row was chosen by comparing outcomes. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 27 restricts nickel RELEASE from articles in prolonged skin contact rather than nickel content in a mixture and is not carried in the register as a content limit, so it is not read across; EU REACH Annex XVII entry 72 Appendix 12 sets no nickel row for consumer textiles, so no downstream fabric content ceiling is available for this analyte. EU REACH Annex XVII entry 75 (Regulation (EU) 2020/2081) sets the strictest sovereign consumer-mixture metal limits in the register (lead 0.7, cadmium 0.5, total arsenic 0.5, mercury 0.5, Cr(VI) 0.5 and nickel 5 mg/kg), but its scope is mixtures for tattooing and permanent make-up, an intradermal-injection route. Reading an injected-mixture ceiling across to a rinse-off or fabric-deposited topical product would conflate exposure routes, and its register basis is a tattoo/permanent-make-up mixture basis that this row's basis_guard excludes. Nickel is measured in soaps, detergents and household hygienic products in the routed corpus and is named for laundry care by greenseal2020-gs51-laundry-care-products, nydec2020-cleansing-product-disclosure-bmp and ecetoc1992-tr045-ni-co-cr-consumer-products, so a not-material determination is unavailable on the facts. Complete disposition of the stricter rows the compiler's audit safety-net surfaces for this row, none of which is adopted. (1) de_bvl2017_pb_cosmetics_general_2000 (2 mg/kg), de_bvl2017_cd_cosmetics_general_100 (0.1), de_bvl2017_tas_cosmetics_general_500 (0.5) and de_bvl2017_thg_cosmetics_general_100 (0.1): Germany BVL 2017 orientation values for technically avoidable trace content, register status 'guidance impurity limit', not statutory maximum levels, and therefore not selectable under Method v2.0. (2) hc_cosmetic_cd_3ppm (3 mg/kg): a Health Canada cosmetic impurity guidance limit, excluded for the same reason, and adopting it for cadmium alone would additionally mix regimes. (3) eu_2018_1513_pb_consumer_textiles_1mgkg, eu_2018_1513_cd_consumer_textiles_1mgkg and eu_2018_1513_tas_consumer_textiles_1mgkg (1 mg/kg each): the EU consumer-textile restriction, which governs the FABRIC and not the product applied to it, on a homogeneous-material-after-extraction basis rather than this row's product-content basis; carried as a downstream constraint and companion limit, and blocked from anchoring by the row's basis_guard. The audit_exclude_scope on this row removes only the oral/mucosal toothpaste tier, tattoo and permanent-make-up mixtures, and colour-additive and colourant specifications; against the register as built it suppresses no row that the audit scope would otherwise surface, so nothing was excluded to make this list shorter. (4) Two further stricter rows exist that the compiler's audit safety-net structurally CANNOT surface, because it filters to the credible-regulator set, and they are therefore recorded by hand rather than left invisible. asean2019_th_cd_cosmetic_products_3000 (Thailand, cadmium 3 mg/kg) is a guidance impurity limit and falls to the same reasoning as the Health Canada value. eaeu_trcu009_2011_pb_natural_raw_material_cosmetics_5mgkg (EAEU, lead 5 mg/kg) is the only STATUTORY row anywhere in the consumer-chemical product-content basis family that is stricter than an anchor adopted here, and it is not adopted for three reasons: the EAEU is not in the HMTc credible-regulator set and admitting it is a governance ruling outside this derivation; its scope is formulation-conditioned (perfumery-cosmetic products containing more than 1 per cent natural plant or natural mineral raw material) rather than the general product tier, and that condition would be satisfied by some but not all rows in this category, which would fracture the category on a formulation test no sovereign wrote for laundry products; and the EAEU instrument does not dominate the adopted regime, since it is stricter on lead (5 against 10 mg/kg) but LOOSER on total arsenic (5 against 2 mg/kg), identical on mercury and silent on cadmium, so taking its lead value while keeping the Chinese arsenic value would compose a ceiling no sovereign enforces, which is precisely what the single-coherent-regime discipline exists to prevent. This enumeration is the output of a credible-set-blind sweep of every one of the 80 register rows the basis_guard admits, not of the automated scan alone, and it is complete as of this build. Verification method: total nickel by microwave-assisted closed-vessel acid digestion (HNO3/H2O2, with a hydrofluoric-acid or lithium-metaborate fusion step where an aluminosilicate, silicate or clay mineral phase is present, which is the normal case on this row) followed by ICP-MS quantification against matrix-matched calibration standards, with a certified reference material and a reagent blank in every run. The sampling unit is one composite sample per production lot, drawn from the product as placed on the market, with component-level sampling of every physical component and functional phase of the article, enumerated in the applicant's Step 0 scope determination and each sampled and tested as a distinct component; solid and sheet formats are separated into substrate and applied coating. A lot at or above the published ceiling fails; a lot inside the ceiling but outside the running per-row control band is escalated to ALARA review with raw-material trace-back.
